## 2026-11-04 — P0 Sandbox GET 401 cache invalidation / token rotation (offline verified)
- **Estimated whole-product completion 87% (+1pp from 86%); RELEASE HOLD.** This percentage is an engineering estimate of verified subsystem progress, **not live production readiness**.
- **Verified feature/test SHA `50e2f15febe2cab350cbd861e009e4764e4e5716`**, branch `lead/paypal`. Updated `src/worker_pinned_paypal_sandbox_get.mjs` and `src/worker_pinned_paypal_sandbox_oauth.mjs`; expanded `tests/worker_pinned_paypal_sandbox_oauth.test.mjs` by four tests. No backend payment claim/approval, Part 1/2 UI, schemas, or production settings changed.
- **Fixed defect:** A PayPal Orders GET HTTP 401 previously left an invalid cached OAuth Bearer token for the next manual request. The pinned GET now invalidates *only that rejected token* through the composed OAuth cache, without an automatic token refresh, GET retry or PayPal CREATE/CAPTURE. HTTP 403 does not force reissuance (permission failure is distinct from token invalidation). A late 401 from an earlier GET cannot evict a newer cached token. Optional invalidator is validated; cleanup exception cannot turn a rejected GET into success. This prevents repeated stale-token failures with no unapproved network retries. Selected selective 401 eviction over automatic retry (better provider cost, predictability and explicit-owner control) and over unconditional cache reset (safe concurrency).
- **Executed local Node 22.16 focused suite:** `cd /mnt/data/paypal_claim && node --experimental-sqlite --test tests/*.test.mjs` **94 PASS / 0 FAIL / 0 SKIP**, exit 0; prior baseline **90 PASS**; `node --check` for 3 touched files PASS. New cases cover explicit retry after 401, 403 behavior, stale-token race and invalid invalidator config.
- **Limits:** All HTTP responses and credentials are synthetic; isolated project subtree relies on local stubs for unrelated Worker host dependencies. **Full repo GitHub Windows/Linux CI, Workerd/Wrangler, real Cloudflare D1, actual PayPal Sandbox OAuth/Orders GET, real IdP and Production remain UNVERIFIED** on this SHA. The new GitHub test also covers opt-in router, but full-tree execution/CI and hosted Workerd+D1 remain **UNVERIFIED**.

> **2026-11-01 KST — PERSISTED BROWSER-BOUND OIDC STATE/NONCE/PKCE S256 / 774/774 PASS:** Implemented `src/oidc_login_challenge_store.mjs` and `tests/oidc_login_challenge_store.test.mjs` to close the real server-side missing link between pinned RS256 ID-token verifier and hashed host sessions. The new opt-in, private Node SQLite adapter requires a caller-owned 32-byte secret, fixed same-origin HTTPS start POST and callback GET, explicit Origin check on start, random 256-bit state/nonce/PKCE verifier/browser binding, PKCE SHA256 S256 challenge, hashed state and browser binding at rest, and AES-256-GCM encrypted private nonce/verifier in a separate DB. One-time callback requires matching `SameSite=Lax; Secure; HttpOnly; __Host-` browser cookie (Lax needed for IdP top-level redirect), matching state, bounded authorization code, issuer check if supplied, non-expired/unconsumed row, and `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` atomic consume. Wrong browser, replay/restart, expired challenge, wrong decryption key/altered AES tag, duplicate callback params and forged issuer fail closed. The private consume result is **not a token exchange**: a future authorized OAuth host must send the recovered PKCE verifier and code to its trusted configured IdP endpoint, then verify the returned ID token against the stored expectedNonce and establish a host session; this turn made **ZERO provider calls** and **NO public HTTP server**. Verified RSA-signed fake IdP token→nonce→old `createPinnedOidcIdTokenVerifier`→durable `createHostVerifiedSessionStore` in tests.
